## 2026-09-20 — Initial feasibility assessment [NB-0003]

- **Question:** the user asked for the assistant's own assessment of whether decipherment is possible.
- **Scope:** discussion and a limited factual source check; the research-execution phase remains inactive. Consulted Yale's manuscript overview, a Yale interview with Claire Bowern, and the bibliographic record/introduction of Reddy and Knight (2011). Source records: SRC-0003 through SRC-0005. No systematic literature review or experiments performed.
- **Source observation:** Yale continues to describe the text as undeciphered. Bowern emphasizes how many assumptions theories require while distinguishing structural knowledge from semantic understanding. These are source reports, not independent replication.
- **Assistant assessment, not an experimental finding:** full decipherment is possible but cannot responsibly be called likely from present evidence. A constrained, consistent encoding with surviving redundancy would offer a better prospect than arbitrary private conventions or text without recoverable semantic content. More capable models cannot uniquely recover information absent from the evidence.
- **Method assessment:** synthetic ground truth combined with causal model analysis is the most compelling proposal to investigate, without claiming novelty or demonstrated transfer. A learned predictor can reveal useful structure without recovering the historical generator or meanings. Unrestricted context-dependent mappings and filler exceptions would make a proposed decoding unfalsifiable.
- **Evidence that would increase confidence:** a compact frozen mechanism or decoder making independently checkable predictions on excluded manuscript material; progressively stronger evidence would be required for semantic claims. No numerical success probability assigned.
- **Validation / checkpoint:** source links and attribution scope recorded; preparation-only status preserved; documentation link and whitespace checks accompany this checkpoint. Commit and push outcome is verified in the session's tool record.
- **Next state:** continue discussion or await instruction to begin; no direction selected for execution and no resources spent on training or paid research API calls.

## 2026-09-20 — Reader usability and mechanistic interpretation [NB-0004]

- **User position:** cumbersome private codebook lookup seems unlikely for an everyday reference; the user remains particularly optimistic about mechanistic interpretability.
- **Limited source check:** SRC-0006 reports Davis's assessment of wear consistent with repeated use. This supports the user's premise in qualified form; it does not establish daily-use consensus or a particular encoding.
- **Assistant clarification:** the arbitrary private-codebook example was a limiting case for identifiability, not a favored historical explanation. Frequent use would disfavor continual expensive lookup, conditional on that use being established. It would not exclude learned private conventions, memorized vocabulary, or shared abbreviations.
- **Working methodological inference:** reader learnability can help prioritize compact, reusable candidate rules. Ease for a trained reader does not guarantee recoverability for an outsider. This is a modeling preference, not a new observed manuscript property.
- **Potential interpretability question:** whether different surface forms converge on a common internal representation that causally supports predictions across contexts. Such a representation would still need synthetic calibration, competing generator controls, and independent validation before being interpreted as a historical or semantic unit.
- **Validation / checkpoint:** preparation-only state retained, no experiment executed; documentation links and whitespace checked before the discussion checkpoint. Git publication verified in the session tool record.
- **Next state:** continue discussion; mechanistic interpretability is a user preference, not yet an authorized execution task.

## 2026-09-20 — Proposed first modeling sequence [NB-0005]

- **Question:** whether the first step toward mechanistic interpretation is to build a model architecture and train it exclusively on essentially the whole manuscript.
- **Proposed sequence, not an executed experiment:** establish a traceable transcription and preserve layout/uncertainty; define development and test pages or blocks before fitting; initialize a small conventional decoder-only transformer from random weights; train next-unit prediction on the training portion of Voynich alone; check generalization against simple baselines; then apply causal interpretation and synthetic calibration.
- **Architecture clarification:** a custom architecture is not a prerequisite. Start with an architecture that is straightforward to inspect, and choose size after corpus inspection. Character/transcription-unit modeling is a candidate starting point; do not silently equate transcription characters with historical glyphs or assume spaces are plaintext word boundaries.
- **Evaluation clarification:** exclusive Voynich training does not require exposing every manuscript page during the first run. Reserve validation material for choices and an untouched test set for final evaluation, checking repeated material and overlapping windows for leakage. A later full-corpus exploratory model is possible, but its training pages can no longer serve as independent evidence of generalization for that model.
- **Modeling purpose:** establish a reproducible predictor with inspectable weights and activations. Prediction quality and mechanistic findings do not alone establish semantics or the historical generator. Save intermediate checkpoints where useful and compare multiple seeds once a minimal pipeline works.
- **Status / validation:** discussion only; no corpus acquired, architecture implemented, framework selected, training started, or API call made for research. Documentation whitespace checked; checkpoint publication verified in the session tool record.

Results written to `outputs/pdf-assessment-2026-09-21/local_corpus_checks.json`.
  - Tokens 36,292; types 9,236; Currier `L` page tags A=114, B=83, ?=29.
  - Top word overall `daiin` 788 / 2.171%. Language A: `daiin` 4.317% (Bowern cites ~4.5%); B: `chedy` 1.969% (Bowern cites ~2.1% for that form). Adjacent identical-word rate 0.761% (Bowern cites A 0.84% / B 0.94%).
  - Zipf log–log slope on top 500 ranks ≈ −0.890 (Zipf-like under this tokenization). Hapax 72.2% of types.
  - Rough character h2: 2.172 bits with spaces; 2.366 without (spaces stripped and words concatenated). **Not** a replication of their ~250-language comparison; confirms low-ish conditional predictability and A/B vocabulary split directionally, not their absolute cross-linguistic ranking.
  - Top-50 A∩B overlap only 20/50; several A-top forms absent from B-top-50 and vice versa — consistent with Currier A/B as a descriptive partition in this corpus.
- **Web checks (not local experiments):** University of Arizona AMS parchment dating (Hodgins letter / voynich.nu summary): weighted mean calibration commonly reported as **1404–1438 CE at 95%** for sampled leaves — supports Bowern’s parchment-date citation; dates **parchment**, not composition of the text. Beinecke catalog: **~102 surviving folios**, historical foliation through 116 with missing leaves; Bowern’s “116 folios (232 pages)” is a common loose gloss, not the surviving leaf count. Timm & Schinner-type critiques (lingbuzz commentary on the Annual Review paper) and Rugg & Taylor (2016) argue language-like statistics can arise from structured nonsemantic generation — so Bowern’s “encoded natural language” conclusion remains **contested**, not field consensus.
- **Observations — PDF1 (Bowern & Lindemann):** Careful about undeciphered status and no language consensus; explicit rejections of Gibbs abbreviated Latin, Hauer & Kondrak Hebrew, Nahuatl chronologies, and Bax as unproven. Stronger leaps: treating higher-level structure as nearly decisive against *all* gibberish/hoax accounts; “should have been solved if simple substitution”; undergraduate nonsense-writing anecdote as evidence. Programmatic recommendation (non-1:1 but regular, predictability-inducing encodings; preserve Zipf; separate A/B) is useful as **modeling preference**, not historical truth.
- **Observations — PDF2 (D’Imperio 1978):** Surveys claimed solutions (Newbold, Feely, Strong, Brumbaugh, etc.) without endorsing a solve. Still-useful negatives from that cryptanalytic tradition: simple 1:1 substitution on unaltered NL is a poor fit; classical polyalphabetic flattening fights the observed repetition; Currier A/B / multi-scribe; positional begin–mid–end structure. Explicitly **dated**/superseded: pre-C14 date scatter, Bacon/13th-c. authorship story as conjecture, absence of scientific pigment/parchment study (true in 1978 only), photocopy-era corpus limits. Preferred historical hypotheses (abbreviated Latin ± nulls; synthetic root+ending language; message in dummy) are **author/era theories**, not facts.
- **Interpretation / verdicts:**
  - **PDF1 relevance:** High as a linguistics-methods survey and constraint checklist; medium for *next* synthetic-recovery experiments (already partly absorbed via entropy prior work and charter filler/homophony hypotheses). Does **not** authorize dropping structured-nonsemantic controls.
  - **PDF1 truthfulness:** Generally careful on failures of specific decipherments; **less careful** when the abstract and summary assert natural language over hoax as the working conclusion. Grade the NL claim as **author theory / contested empirical**, not truth.
  - **PDF1 accuracy:** Background (Beinecke MS 408; C14 parchment window; undeciphered; no language consensus; A/B frequency differences; Zipf-like ranks; low character predictability under EVA) largely holds or holds directionally on our ZL3b check. Folio “116 = 232 pages” oversimplified. Cross-linguistic entropy uniqueness and “hoax ruled out” are **not** independently established here.
  - **PDF2 relevance:** Medium–low for current architecture/causal work; medium as historical map of failed claims and early cryptanalytic negatives. Garbled OCR should not drive modeling.
  - **PDF2 truthfulness:** Survey tone; does not claim a personal decipherment. Theories of others are presented as surveyed claims.
  - **PDF2 accuracy:** Identity and chapter structure confirmed against NSA/WorldCat. OCR cannot be trusted for quotes, counts, or alphabets. Physical-science and dating sections are outdated relative to post-2009 evidence.
- **Adopt as soft constraints (not historical truths):** Keep Currier A/B (and hands) as analysis partitions; do not assume 1:1 grapheme↔phoneme; retain Zipf-preserving and high local predictability as properties candidate generators should *match*, including structured nonsemantic generators; continue rejecting surveyed language-ID “solutions” as established. **Ignore / quarantine:** any reading of either PDF as proving natural language, proving a hoax, identifying Latin/Hebrew/Nahuatl/etc., or supplying a translation.
- **Working hypotheses (assistant proposals; not discoveries):** see parent report. Each requires a falsifier; none is evidenced by these PDFs alone.
- **Limitations:** No training; no paid API; no full re-replication of Bowern/Lindemann multi-language entropy tables; D’Imperio assessed primarily via local OCR plus NSA identity confirmation, not a clean typeset reread of every chapter; deep-review sources.json not rewritten.

## 2026-09-21 — Structured gibberish surface-stat comparison [NB-0022]

- **Phase / question:** Which surface statistics of ZL3b can a small structured nonsemantic generator match, and which still separate it from the manuscript? Linked to HYP-005 as an exploratory control comparison (not a confirmatory resolution of HYP-001–006). Dead hypothesis refused: pure iid random text as an account of the manuscript — already inconsistent with Zipf-like ranks, repeated words, and low character entropy (NB-0021). This iteration tests **structured** gibberish only, with iid kept solely as a negative control expected to fail.
- **Status at this write:** **Preregistration only.** Generators, metrics, match/separation rule, and splits fixed below before any generator sample metrics are inspected.
- **Data split (frozen; test untouched):** Official ZL3b `data/processed/zl3b/{train,validation,test}.jsonl`. Fit generator parameters and character/word inventories on **train only**. Emit synthetic corpora sized to **validation** token/page structure. Score reference metrics on **validation**; report train metrics only as the natural train↔val fluctuation scale for the match rule. Do **not** open test for scoring or fitting.
- **Generators (preregistered):**
  1. **`iid_char` (negative control):** sample each character independently from the train unigram over the cleaned EVA alphabet plus space; insert newlines to roughly match validation line counts per page. Expect separation on Zipf slope, adjacent-repeat rate, and h2.
  2. **`selfcite_ab` (structured nonsemantic):** simplified Timm & Schinner–style self-citation / copy–mutate process (Cryptologia 2020 argument; accompanying generator materials cited as D07 / TorstenTimm SelfCitationTextgenerator). **Simplifications vs published full generator (explicit):** (a) no folio geometry or illustration-conditioned layout; (b) no published similarity-network parameters or verbatim port of their Java/codebase; (c) words chosen from a recent buffer with copy / single-edit mutate / prefix–suffix splice, else a fresh draw from a train-fitted character bigram + length histogram; (d) two style buffers **A** and **B** fitted from Currier `L`∈{A,B} train pages (pages with other/unknown `L` excluded from style-specific fits; generated validation-shaped pages use the validation page’s `L` when A/B, else draw style from train A/B page proportions). Seed fixed below.
- **Reuse:** Clean/tokenize and metric formulas follow NB-0021 local checks (PUA stripped; EVA letters + space; spaces as word breaks). Existing `voynich.synthetic` remains the EXP-0004 toy alphabet and is **not** reused as a Voynich-shaped null. No parallel neural framework.
- **Preregistered metrics (all on cleaned space-delimited words / character streams):**
  1. `zipf_slope_top500` — OLS slope of log freq vs log rank on top min(500, V) types.
  2. `adjacent_repeat_rate` — fraction of consecutive word pairs that are identical.
  3. `char_h2_bits` — empirical next-character conditional entropy H(X_t | X_{t-1}) in bits, spaces retained.
  4. `ab_top50_jaccard` — Jaccard overlap of top-50 word types between Currier A vs B pages (pages with `L`∉{A,B} excluded from this metric).
  5. `top1_word_share` — relative frequency of the single most common word type (overall).
- **Cheap positional metric (preregistered):** `line_initial_type_share` — fraction of line-initial words accounted for by the top-20 line-initial types (lines = newline-separated loci text). Included because it is cheap and already implicit in page line structure.
- **Match / separation rule (preregistered, applied per metric on validation-sized samples):** Let R = metric(validation), T = metric(train), G = metric(generator sample). Tolerance τ = max(|T − R|, floor[m]) with floors: zipf_slope 0.15; adjacent_repeat_rate 0.005; char_h2_bits 0.25; ab_top50_jaccard 0.15; top1_word_share 0.010; line_initial_type_share 0.05. **Match** if |G − R| ≤ τ; **separate** if |G − R| > τ. Overall structured-generator verdict: **matched** if ≥4/6 metrics match; **separated** if ≤2/6 match; else **inconclusive**. The iid control is successful as a control if it separates on ≥4/6. This run does **not** resolve HYP-005 even on a full match (matching selected surface stats ≠ proving historical nonsemantic production).
- **Seeds / bounds:** `generator_seed = 20260921`; one sample per generator; wall-clock budget a few minutes; no paid API; no neural training.
- **Output path (planned):** `outputs/NB-0022-structured-gibberish/comparison.json` (Git-ignored `outputs/`).
- **Falsifier for this structured-gibberish *control* claim:** if `selfcite_ab` separates on the majority of preregistered metrics under this rule, this simplified generator fails as a sufficient surface-stat null for those metrics (does not prove the manuscript is language).
- **Exploratory extras (if computed, non-deciding):** hapax fraction, A/B top-1 word identity strings, mean word length — labeled exploratory only.
- **Git:** no commit/push for this iteration (user instruction).

## 2026-09-21 — Latent message recovery under cipher + structured nulls [NB-0023]

- **Phase / question:** Can a small structural model recover a coherent latent message from an unknown writing system contaminated by structured pseudolanguage? User objective for this session: full decipherment pathway; synthetic held-out-language benchmark is the gate before any Voynich label-free run. Linked: HYP-001, HYP-002, M-001. Explicitly **not** a resumption of NB-0022 structured-gibberish surface matching (that track was aborted).

- **Pipeline:** per sample `L → C(L) → N(C(L))` with a **new** cipher mapping every sample. Worlds A (pure language), B (cipher, no nulls), C (cipher+nulls), D (pure structured pseudotext). Filler families: random_char, random_pseudoword, copy_mutate, periodic, shift_phase, stateful. Primary filler rate **0.30**.
- **Languages / licenses (planned download):** English PG#11 Alice (PG license / PD USA); Latin PG#218 Caesar *De Bello Gallico* I–IV (Latin; PG license / PD USA); held-out Finnish PG#7000 *Kalevala* 1849 (Finnish; PG license / PD USA). Cleaner folds to `a-z` + space (Finnish diacritics stripped; syntax/vocab remain Finnish). Manifest: `data/manifests/exp0011_corpora.json`.
- **Models:** Tiny bidirectional LSTM + mask/recon/world heads (target ≪100k params; exact count recorded at run). Classical 2-state copy/rank Viterbi beside it. No paid API; MPS if available else CPU; max 3,000 updates.
- **Baselines:** majority/delete-nothing; matched-rate random deletion (20 seeds); training world-B/C surface-token vocab filter (must stay weak); classical null model.
- **Preregistered PASS (Finnish world-C holdout, seeds data=4011 / model=42 / finnish=4012):** either neural or classical with (1) mask_f1≥0.65; (2) mask_acc≥majority_acc+0.08; (3) mask_f1≥matched_random_f1+0.08; (4) mask_f1≥vocab_filter_f1+0.05; (5) vocab_filter_f1≤0.55 else FAIL cheat-surface; (6) recon_acc≥matched_random_recon+0.08; (7) pred_bits_gain≥matched_random_gain+0.05. Thresholds not to be moved after seeing numbers. One bounded fix+rerun allowed on FAIL.
- **Voynich gate:** only if PASS; label-free structure test on ZL3b **validation** only (glyph and token segmentations); **test split untouched**. Positive structure ≠ decipherment.
- **Simplifications (explicit):** reconstruct `C(L)` via deletion, not full plaintext cryptanalysis; classical is 2-state feature Viterbi not full 20-state HSMM; single scored filler rate 0.30.
- **Commands (planned):** `.venv/bin/python -m voynich.latent_recovery --root . --device auto --voynich-if-pass`

- **Question:** How have AI/LLM systems actually made progress on Navier–Stokes, Erdős-type problems, and formal math, and which mechanisms (not headlines) are worth trying for decipherment here? Charter untouched. Complementary to deep-review and world-models memos; does not launch experiments.
- **Actions:** Read MEMORY, latest notebook through NB-0030, PROTOCOL, HYPOTHESES, BACKLOG, PRIOR_WORK, NEXT_DESIGN, DECIPHERMENT, world-models SYNTHESIS. Fetched primary web sources via Cursor WebSearch/WebFetch (Clay PDFs/pages, OpenAI NS PDF, Nature news, DeepMind blogs, FunSearch/AlphaTensor Nature pages, AlphaEvolve arXiv, Tao blog, AlphaGeometry/AlphaProof papers/blogs, PINN/FNO/DeepONet/SINDy/AI Feynman, Copiale, Reddy–Knight, Hauer–Kondrak, Conneau–Lample, Goldwater et al.). Wrote `docs/research/ai-hard-problems-2026-09-21/README.md` and `SOURCES.md`. Updated MEMORY (NS status + reading map) and knowledge INDEX pointer only.
- **Exact inputs (sources):** listed in `SOURCES.md`. Key URLs: Clay navierstokes.pdf; OpenAI `cdn.openai.com` NS PDF; Nature d41586-026-02842-5; CMI 11 Sep 2026 announcement and prize rules page; FunSearch Nature 2023 + DeepMind blog; AlphaTensor Nature 2022; AlphaEvolve arXiv:2506.13131 and 2511.02864; Tao 2025-11-05; DeepMind IMO 2024 blog; AlphaGeometry Nature 2024; AG2 arXiv:2502.03544; Raissi 2019 PINN PDF copy; Krishnapriyan arXiv:2109.01050; Li et al. FNO OpenReview PDF; Copiale ACL W11-1202.pdf; Reddy–Knight W11-1511.pdf.
- **Observations (sourced; not our experiments):**
  - Clay NS prize is one of A/B (unforced global regularity) **or** C/D (breakdown, possibly with force). OpenAI Thm 1.1 is **forced** blowup from rest with compact force — C/D-direction, **not** A/B. CMI said the problem “apparently been settled” but prize rules still require qualifying publication, ≥2 years, general acceptance. OpenAI HTML blog **403**. Lean not compiled here.
  - PINN/FNO/DeepONet/SINDy/AI Feynman are surrogates or equation discovery in regimes, **not** Clay proofs. PINNs can fail via ill-conditioned soft residuals (Krishnapriyan 2021).
  - FunSearch: new cap-set **constructions** (incl. 512 in \(n=8\)) + bin-packing heuristics, evaluator-in-the-loop. AlphaTensor: better small-matrix algorithms, not \(\omega\). AlphaEvolve: many construction problems; slight Erdős **minimum overlap** upper bound improvement; Tao et al. report failures too.
  - AlphaProof/AG2: IMO 2024 4/6, 28/42, Lean after human formalization, multi-day TTRL; combinatorics unsolved. Informal Gemini 2025 contest gold is graded NL, not a research-problem kernel.
  - Copiale: failed “Romans are content / others nulls”; succeeded when Romans were **spaces** and colon doubled consonants; independent German check. Hauer–Kondrak: strong synthetic ciphers; Voynich Hebrew **suggestion** under anagram assumptions, authors flag artifacts.
- **Interpretation:** Transferable pattern is **proposal + ungameable verifier**, not scale or fluency. Voynich lacks a known PDE and a Mathlib of meanings; a neural EVA generator is the analogue of an FNO, not of a theorem. ## 2026-09-23 — NB-TEACH-52: preliminary 24.77M candidate-edge workspace interfaces

**Question.** Can the proposed raw parser, explicit address/value memory and repeated reader be implemented with intervention points before optimization, while preventing two common control leaks: the parser seeing the query and the oracle arm receiving hidden stage order?

**Actions.** Added `src/voynich/workspace/teacher14_models.py` as a design-phase model, with six bidirectional width512/8-head/FF1536 encoder blocks, two candidate-set blocks, all adjacent ordinary-symbol pairs as candidate edges (up to63 for32 rows), learned edge gates, separate key/value projections and up to four shared address→read→state transitions. Parameter count is24,766,465. Each raw parser block sees the episode body only; task/query suffix enters only the reader. The oracle variant verifies that row tensors equal the visible strip-and-pair parser in physical serialization order before use. Initial `one_read` and `mean_address` ablations are present. Hooks expose encoder Q/K/V, parser gates, memory K/V, per-hop address/read/state and answer logits; interventions require exact shape and device.

**Architecture correction.** The earlier design's40 fixed slots would require a separate aggregation step and could hide edge selection errors. The implemented parser keeps all63 adjacent-pair candidates for the maximum32-row graph and learns gates for true versus cross-row pairs. Their downstream read is permutation-invariant when candidates and K/V/gates are permuted together. This is an overcomplete candidate-edge set rather than unsupervised object discovery. No nonoverlap penalty is yet implemented; its absence must be diagnosed or prospectively corrected before source freeze.

**Validation and limits.** A random two-episode CPU forward/backward produced finite logits and gradients. Five focused model tests pass, including finite raw/oracle backward on mixed row counts, exact parser-state invariance to task/query changes, exact identity intervention, permutation-invariant read, query-independent-address behavior, and explicit rejection of hidden stage-order oracle rows. Changed-file Ruff and diff hygiene pass. These are interface checks on random weights. No optimizer campaign, qualified behavior, diffusion result, causal support or Voynich inference exists. Next: finalize structural edge competition, training/suite/auditor and source-matched resource benchmark.

## 2026-09-23 — NB-TEACH-54: matched dense and finite interchange training interfaces

**Question.** Can the proposed model comparison isolate explicit K/V routing from parameter count and can a correct-versus-wrong causal objective exercise recipient-specific reuse of a first-read state without changing input exposure?

Both see the same visible full episode, but measured block applications, wall time and backward cost remain to be benchmarked. Added `public_edge_loss`, which labels true even-indexed candidate pairs from the deterministic visible grammar; this is explicit grammar supervision and a ceiling for the answer-only parser, not hidden-structure recovery. `CandidateEdgeWorkspace` now returns its differentiable first-read state and edge logits without retaining full Q/K/V traces on every training step.

Added `causal_pair`/`causal_training_batch`: a donor `(F1,G0)` and bases `(F0,G0)`/`(F0,G1)` share one query and distractor graph. The same donor first-hop key has recipient targets `G0(k1)` and `G1(k1)`, guaranteed distinct from each other and both base answers. All four graph variants are in train/train stage families. `interchange_loss` inserts the donor's `query.1` state into both bases. The wrong-target control cyclically deranges targets across groups while preserving each G-recipient cell's label multiset; inputs and patched logits are identical, so a gain is attributable to target correspondence rather than extra forward paths. This remains supervised synthetic causal training, not a mechanism result.

## 2026-09-23 — NB-TEACH-55: matched four-step edge denoising interfaces

**Question and source basis.** Can the optional discrete absorbing-mask edge refiner be compared with a recurrent refiner without giving it more parameters, public-grammar labels or model evaluations? The D3PM source and transfer limit are recorded in `docs/experiments/TEACH-0014-design.md`. This implements a supervised discrete corruption/refinement test on generated edges, not a text diffusion model or an evidence-backed Voynich decipherment method.

**Actions and exact inputs.** Added `recurrent4` and `diffusion4` variants to the same candidate-edge workspace. Both use the existing six-block raw encoder, two candidate-set blocks, the same candidate features, a shared four-application refiner block, a three-state edge embedding (false/true/MASK), the same K/V reader and exactly27,390,465 parameters. Both expose edge logits after each of four refiner applications. `diffusion4` starts with every valid candidate masked, then reveals the most confident previously masked candidates to reach one-quarter, one-half, three-quarters and all valid candidates. Once revealed, a decision remains fixed; the downstream gate is the logit captured at its reveal step. `recurrent4` keeps the edge state masked through all four applications and refines continuous features only. During future training, **both** must receive one additional q_t-conditioned evaluation with public even/odd edge labels, where each true class is independently replaced by MASK with probability t/4 at registered t∈{1,2,3,4}; the loss is on masked positions only. This makes the intended difference discrete feedback at answer inference, not access to labels or an extra denoising pass. `matched_refinement_objectives` returns the same answer, four-step edge and q_t-denoising loss components for both. Loss weights and timestep seed schedule remain unfrozen; there has been no optimizer update.

**Validation and limitations.** On a fixed generated two-hop/one-distractor episode, identical recurrent/diffusion weights produce exactly identical first-step edge logits and identical q_t-denoising loss under identical corruption uniforms; later step logits differ after discrete feedback. The diffusion state is fully unmasked after four steps and its final binary decisions equal the signs of retained gate logits. CPU tests cover finite outputs, four cached applications, denoising backward gradients and invalid inputs; the12 focused model/objective tests, full repository suite (953 passed,8 skipped,23 subtests passed), changed-file Ruff and diff hygiene pass. This is an interface check at random weights, not a performance or diffusion-advantage result. Amended the design from nine to ten main arms because the two-step edge-aux arm is not a fair four-step control; the eight-hour candidate envelope must be recalculated before launch. The action-transition branch, source freeze, complete outcome auditor, fresh confirmation suite and24-update MPS benchmark are still pending. No TEACH-0014 training or manuscript inference occurred.

## 2026-09-24 — NB-TEACH-73: full finite key-transfer assay implementation before outcomes

**Question and methodological basis.** Can the prospective TEACH-0015 unchanged `query.1` donor state be tested across all three G-remapped recipients with source-frozen, full-denominator, independently checkable controls rather than a handful of persuasive examples? **Prospective correction.** An initial per-group wrong/deranged donor rule reused donors unevenly (only86/128 unique wrong donors in discovery and80/128 in confirmation), contradicting the stated donor-distribution control. ## 2026-09-24 — NB-TEACH-82: four-bit visible relative-rank rival

**Question and insight.** The absolute physical-row pointer fails TEACH-0016 shifted-order pairs, but does the new challenge identify a globally stable key-token state? A visible structural scan, before any trained TEACH-0015/16 inference, found that the **set of row left operands** is identical between source and recipient in all9,216/9,216 attempts in **each** TEACH-0016 split. F0/F1 swap right operands, G0/G1/G2 remap right operands, and serialization reorders rows; none changes this set. The local `METHODS_REVIEW.md`, [Geiger et al. 2024](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v236/geiger24a.html) and [Bing et al. 2024](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v236/bing24a.html) caution that interchange success and causal representation identification depend on the tested abstraction and intervention diversity. The relative-rank construction below is our own mathematical counterexample, not a result claimed by those papers.

**Method and exact inputs.** Wrote `TEACH-0016-relative-rank-counterexample.md`, a visible-token-only symbolic constructor and a separate metadata-based no-generator auditor. The constructor strips public pair rows from `Episode.tokens`, follows the queried first hop to `k`, then carries only `rank(k; sorted visible row left operands)` as intermediate state. The recipient uses that ordinal to select its own left operand and follows its row. The state is an integer0--15 across the full observed suites, never the original symbol ID; the decoder uses the current visible program. The same global split-wide distinct-group permutation used by the neural assay supplies its matched wrong-group donor; F0 same-group gives wrong-key, a SHA-256-selected ordinal gives a symbolic random null, and source final answer is a direct-injection control. This random ordinal is **not** the neural norm-matched512-vector, so this construction is a behavioral/identifiability rival only and cannot pass the numerical checkpoint gate.

## 2026-09-24 — NB-TEACH-87: query-relative algebraic rival to global-key identification

**Question and derivation.** The fresh TEACH-0017 suite changes distractor left operands enough to defeat the four-bit rank address, but its donor and recipient query token remains identical. Can a context-dependent code carry the effective intermediate key without carrying a global token coordinate? Proposed the elementary visible-only state `c=(first_hop_key - source_query) mod 2048`; the recipient reconstructs `key=(c + recipient_query) mod 2048` and follows its visible row. When the two query tokens match, the reconstructed key is exact, independently of rank, order, markers, distractors or G remapping. The same key under a changed query has a different code. This is our own algebraic counterexample; the general causal-identifiability literature linked in `TEACH-0017-query-relative-counterexample.md` does not claim it. It is compatible with the TEACH-0017 design's prior warning that same-group successes need not identify a global key.

**Actions and exact inputs.** Wrote the separate prospective counterexample document, visible-token evaluator and metadata-based independent auditor, without editing the source-bound TEACH-0017 suite design or raw manifests. Inputs are the already source-frozen TEACH-0017 discovery/confirmation128-group manifests, seeds87111/87121, canonical hashes `1984a91a…` and `8be702ae…`. The evaluator strips public pair rows directly from visible tokens, extracts the query at the visible tail, and computes the offset without reading `key1` metadata. It retains all eight source surfaces and3×3 G pairs, pairing source `d` with recipient `1-d` and reversing order. Wrong-key, same-key, globally deranged distinct-group and SHA-selected random-offset controls are explicit. An absent decoded key row is recorded as `-1`, not a fabricated symbol. The independent checker reconstructs expected codes/outputs from serialized-row metadata, verifies every archive row and the committed-source/raw-suite hashes. A pre-freeze exploratory all-cell comparison of evaluator to checker passed18,432/18,432 rows; these numbers informed no fitted model or threshold.

**Exploratory structural result.** Every9,216/9,216 pair in each split has an equal source/recipient query. On the prospectively selected shifted-rank off-diagonal subsets, the offset program transfers4,128/4,128 discovery and4,464/4,464 confirmation targets, while the visible rank program hits0/4,128 and0/4,464. Same-key, identity and reverse controls each hit every selected case; wrong-key, distinct-group and source-final-answer controls hit zero; a symbolic random-offset control hits3/4,128 and5/4,464. The offset code is11 bits and deliberately **not** a norm-matched512-dimensional neural vector, so this is a behavioral identifiability rival, not neural mechanism evidence. The new tests passed3/3 and the full repository suite passed1,025 with8 skipped and23 subtests in67.54s; changed-file Ruff and diff hygiene passed. This exploratory run preceded source freeze. Next: commit the source, run the report and fully independent source-bound audit, then require changed-query same-key pairs in any later cross-group challenge. TEACH-0014 training remains the gate for neural inference; no checkpoint score or Voynich reading was opened.

## 2026-09-24 — NB-TEACH-89: cross-query gauge and collision design

**Question and theory.** After the source-frozen query-relative counterexample, what paired interventions would actually distinguish a globally portable key-equivalent state from `k-q` and the earlier rank/row-pointer codes? Wrote `docs/experiments/TEACH-0019-cross-query-identifiability.md`, deriving the finite interchange relation `D_r(E_c(k))=G_r(k)` and treating equal-query test pairs as disconnected coordinate charts. Because all existing paired edges preserve query, the transform `E_c(k)=k-q_c` is observationally indistinguishable from a key code on those edges. A changed-query, same-key cross-graph edge links charts; a different-key donor with the **same offset** as the first donor supplies a direct collision, since both offset codes are equal but require distinct outputs from one fixed recipient. This is an original mathematical design motivated by the previously reviewed causal-identifiability and distributed-alignment papers, not a theorem or neural result from those papers.

**Prospective experiment and controls.** The design specifies recipient `R` and five donor conditions: A same key across different query/graph; B different key with matched offset; C same query/key cross-graph; D different key/offset; and P same graph but changed query, redirecting `R` to a different key as a patch-validity positive. It also crosses misleading rank and physical row slot where feasible, and requires at least two G remaps, disjoint target/source/base outputs, native competence, explicit context/length/answer controls, fresh graph-family partitions, an all-cell exposure audit, source-frozen numerical replay and predeclared resource gates. P must work before a failure of A/B is interpreted; C/P passing but A failing would localize graph-context transport rather than prove the absence of a key variable. A global invertible recoding of key remains intrinsically indistinguishable even after successful cross-query transfer, so the attainable claim is functional portability, not literal key-token neurons.

**Validation and limit.** The document makes no new sample, threshold, model change, training run, checkpoint inference or manuscript claim. Reviewed it against the source-frozen TEACH-0017 design and `docs/research/PROTOCOL.md`, keeping all frozen files untouched; Markdown/diff hygiene checked. This is a prospective bridge to a new synthetic distribution if the primary and conditional neural gates make it worth executing. The completed TEACH-0017 query-relative result was pushed and verified at origin `03c10e7`; the TEACH-0014 v3 training job remained active at the last status check and final outcomes remained unopened.

## 2026-09-24 — NB-TEACH-106: post hoc linked-reader mass and update-size diagnostic

**Question and scope.** The now-audited TEACH-0022 linked reader had perfect first-row attention *argmax* yet failed first-hop, direct and two-hop answers, even with route supervision. To distinguish ranking from value transport without another holdout or training run, reloaded all eight frozen checkpoints on the already exposed seed84411 `composed_confirm_confirm` panel,128 episodes each. `scripts/teacher0022_posthoc_geometry.py` computes native first/second attention target mass, top mass, first read-value norm and first update norm; checks source suite/checkpoint/audit/replay hashes; and reproduces the independent all-item audit's answer and row-hit counts exactly before writing compact `results/TEACH-0022/posthoc-geometry.json`. CPU readout completed in1.784481s; no paid service, new training or manuscript scoring. This exploratory diagnostic was chosen after seeing TEACH-0022's failure, so its contrasts are descriptive, not confirmation.

**Observation and interpretation.** On seed84411, linked first-target mean mass was only0.0676/0.0675 answer-only and0.0678/0.0677 with route labels, barely above uniform1/16=0.0625, despite128/128 first-row argmax. Second-target mass remained~0.073–0.074 and second argmax hits18–26/128. The successful unlinked-route arm assigned0.7481/0.6662 first-target and0.9920/1.0000 second-target mass. The linked arm's learned update scale declined from0.1 to~0.074, while observed first update norm~0.34 exceeded first carried-value norm~0.206. Thus the proposed direct-carry interface did not produce a sharp or dominant first value under the fixed optimization. No one component is isolated as the cause: tying, scale, normalization, gradient dynamics and their interactions all remain candidates. This supports the prior warning that row-rank accuracy is not a retrieval mechanism. Geometry is not independently replayed beyond matching all audited answers/attention hits; TEACH-0025's fresh hard-first intervention carries the causal claim for the original reader. Details in `docs/experiments/TEACH-0022-results.md`.

## 2026-09-24 — NB-107: manuscript word-copy channel registered before scores

**Question and evidence basis.** After a synthetic reader bottleneck was replicated, redirected work to a manuscript-facing test that could decide whether a copy/edit channel merits inclusion in a constrained decoder. Reviewed Timm and Schinner's original self-citation materials, Timm's earlier similar-word argument, Lindemann and Bowern's transcription-sensitive entropy study, ordinary-language cache work, the local EXP-0019 failure and the project's protocol. These sources motivate a model comparison; local similarity alone does not identify meaningless copying or plaintext.

**Prospective setup.** Registered `docs/experiments/EXP-0027.md` before any new scores. Fit a normalized train-word plus character-backoff distribution, an exact-copy cache and a normalized one-edit cache using pinned ZL3b train SHA `49618c7b…` and validation SHA `9bee4149…`; never read final test. Select hyperparameters on 16 deterministic training leaf groups, refit on all 81 training leaves, then score the 10 already-exposed validation leaves once. The primary contrast is exact-cache minus edit-cache bits per certain EVA word, with a 2,000-draw leaf bootstrap, 100 within-page order permutations, and a known inserted-edit positive control. The predeclared support gate requires ≥0.05 bits/word, a positive leaf-bootstrap lower bound, a gain over the 95th permutation percentile, and a positive-control win. This remains exploratory because validation was previously exposed.

**Implementation and pre-score validation.** Added the candidate likelihood runner, separate aggregate/decision auditor, and two tests. The one-edit operation probabilities sum to one even when repeated letters create multiple edit paths; the empty page cache reverts to the base distribution. Focused tests passed 2/2, changed-file Ruff and diff hygiene passed. No EXP-0027 calibration, validation, permutation or positive-control score had been run at this checkpoint. CPU cap is 20 minutes, under 1 GiB new artifacts, no paid API. Next: source-freeze, run, audit, interpret within the stated limits, then publish the checkpoint.

## 2026-09-24 — NB-117: named historical-herbal image anchor triage

**Question and source review.** Asked whether a named, independently dated herbal illustration could supply a stronger external referent than the disputed internal fragment matches. Reviewed Zandbergen's [illustration survey](https://voynich.nu/illustr.html), his [2012 illustrated comparison slides](https://www.voynich.nu/papers/Voynich100_RZ_2012.pdf) (especially pp. 21–25), the Smithsonian [bibliographic record](https://research.si.edu/publication-details/?id=138275) for Alain Touwaide's 2015 essay, and the publicly indexed [BnF Lat. 6823 IIIF manifest URL](https://gallica.bnf.fr/iiif/ark:/12148/btv1b6000517p/manifest.json). This was literature and source-access triage, not a manuscript text experiment. No Voynich text or model score was inspected in this block. A direct BnF fetch was unavailable from the current environment (sandbox DNS failure; web fetch returned an access error); Touwaide's full essay was not accessed, so neither is treated as independently read evidence.

**Observation.** Zandbergen compares Voynich f35v to *edera nigra* / ivy entwining an oak on Paris BnF Lat. 6823 f60r; the slides provide the named historical image and the survey explicitly states that f35v is **not a direct copy**. The same slides show further cross-manuscript detail similarities, including f7v blue marks and f41v with more than one historical plant-name candidate (*pastinaca* / *meu*), but conclude that no complete Voynich herbal drawing was found to copy any of 50 herbals examined. The project split manifest assigns physical leaf f35 to `test`; its Voynich text was deliberately left closed. These are sourced visual-comparison claims, not verified botanical identifications or deciphered names.

**Interpretation and next state.** f35v is a potentially useful *image-only* final-confirmation candidate if a blinded historical-herbal comparison and at least one independent expert agree on the depicted referent before any f35 text is opened. The present evidence is not a known-plaintext crib: visual inspiration can preserve a motif without preserving the plant name, and the available cross-manuscript named examples are too ambiguous for a forced lexical test. First obtain/inspect the BnF original and a larger, independently rated comparison panel with negative decoys; freeze image ratings and a linguistic prediction before any text comparison. Do not promote f35v to a confirmed ivy label or use its reserved text for exploratory tuning.

## 2026-09-24 — NB-124: open-set historical-image data feasibility, no new model score

**Question and source basis.** After HERBAL-CONTROL-0002 showed that pairwise one-to-one matching (32/36) outperformed the three-way rule (28/36), examined whether a genuinely fresh known-answer image panel can test *rejection* of absent chapter classes. The relevant method gap is open-set recognition under visually similar unknowns: [Scheirer et al.'s open-set work](https://www.wjscheirer.com/projects/openset-recognition/) and [Vaze et al.'s open-set benchmark](https://www.robots.ox.ac.uk/~vgg/research/osr/) motivate explicit known/unknown separation and strong closed-set baselines. [Kaoua et al.'s Image Collation dataset](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2108.08109) is closer to historical herbals, but its published pair annotations are not evidence that every unannotated cross-manuscript pair is a negative. This is data/method feasibility research, not a registered score or manuscript claim.

**Local source inspection.** Read the official [Image Collation code/annotation repository](https://github.com/Rykoua/ImageCollation) at commit `6920385f2057d697a93db2c7afc0a134ab6d3bcb`, cloned read-only to `/tmp/voynich-imagecollation-reference`; no external code or assets were copied into Git. Its `D1-D2`, `D1-D3`, `D2-D3` correspondence files contain 295, 524 and 353 one-to-one edges, respectively. Joining all three pair files by their integer crop IDs yields **287 fully cross-annotated, consistent triples**. The `P1-P2`, `P1-P3`, `P2-P3` files have 50, 50 and 51 edges and yield 50 consistent triples. The repo bundles crop annotations but not the full source-page images; its `P2`/`P3` demo contains 59/67 pages, only the first 15 annotated illustration regions in each, corresponding to the first 15 ordered `P2-P3` edges. A missing annotation cannot be used as a definite unknown class. The demo is too small and ordered to stand alone as a broad confirmation panel. No license file was found in the inspected clone; third-party code/images remain outside tracked assets pending rights review.

**Commons inventory attempt and negative result.** Starting from already downloaded Commons category-member JSON for BnF Latin 6823 (197 file pages), BL Egerton 747 (253) and Casanatense 459 (269), attempted 15 read-only `action=query&prop=categories` requests of at most 50 page IDs each, with 0.8-second spacing and a descriptive user agent, to discover chapter-tagged triples without handpicking drawings. The system Python HTTPS client failed certificate validation before any response. A `curl` retry verified the first 10 batches (500/719 file pages), then Commons returned HTTP 429 on batch 11. Because the one-off probe had no partial-response persistence, no complete chapter inventory was saved and no eligible-class count is claimed. Stopped rather than retrying through the rate limit. No crop, feature score, threshold, Voynich image rank or manuscript text was read in this block; compute cost was negligible and no paid API was used.

**Interpretation and next state.** The official annotation graph shows a potentially much larger *positive* control if source images and rights can be resolved, but incomplete correspondence annotation makes naive open-set negatives invalid. Commons chapter categories remain a more accessible route to explicit absent-class controls when its API is available; the next inventory attempt should persist each batch, respect rate limits, then prospectively select nonoverlapping chapter/page groups before images or model scores are viewed. A fair assay must compare independent pairwise assignment and three-way matching with the same reject option, plus near-unknown distractors and frozen calibration classes. The narrow HERBAL-CONTROL-0002 pass still provides no decipherment or cycle-specific advantage.

## 2026-09-24 — NB-125: 177-class Commons inventory and 72-class open-set source split frozen

**Question and method correction.** Pursued the actual out-of-gallery question exposed by HERBAL-CONTROL-0002: can a historical illustration method reject a chapter absent from a named reference set? Before a new image score, inspected a known Commons file page for BnF Latin 6823 f011v and verified that file-page categories include both *De arthemisia* and *De arthemisia tagantes*. ## 2026-09-24 — NB-127: folio-order confound found before image scoring

**Question and observation.** After pushing the 37-source/37-crop stage-2 checkpoint `24144b98bca0647d2f89b2923e8ef33e3ab17e3d` and verifying `origin/main`, asked whether a manuscript-page-order rule could retrieve the same chapter labels without seeing the drawings. The fixed panel SHA-256 remains `fc2bd19a8eb25d16d8f8387a26b551e42c1f221a048d8f8b07a3f20f02c4b725`. Parsed `f.NNNr/v` title coordinates from **all 72 primary class triplets**, including evaluation identities, as `2N+1[v]`; all 72 folio-side coordinates were unique within each manuscript. Spearman rank correlation was **0.8432 BnF–Egerton, 0.9215 BnF–Casanatense, 0.8490 Casanatense–Egerton**. This is a panel-specific metadata observation, not evidence that Apple Vision literally reads page numbers or a general historical ordering theorem. Critically, inspecting all 72 class pairings **exposed evaluation metadata before the image result**; no evaluation accuracy, image feature or manuscript text was read. The exposure is disclosed rather than hidden.

**Source basis, challenger and claim limit.** The project had already reviewed Kaoua et al.'s historical-herbal collation (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2108.08109) and the prior closed-set control; the new high rank correlations motivated an explicit page-order competitor. `docs/experiments/HERBAL-CONTROL-0003-folio-challenger.md` fixes an ordered-pair Theil–Sen map from the 24 **development-known** matched chapter folios only, raw mapped-folio distance, six development-only pair medians and exactly the image study's four class-cost families, reject-capable batch assignment and threshold grid. The code `src/voynich/herbal_folio_order.py` and `scripts/herbal_control_0003_folio_dev.py` refuses a development score until the full 216-source/216-crop freeze exists; no evaluation scorer was implemented or run. A folio-only result may conservatively veto a visual-specific value claim; it cannot rescue a failed image gate. The metadata-exposed control is not passed off as chosen before panel inspection. Strong chapter-order matching would still leave actual picture matching and Voynich word meaning unidentified.

**Resource and software controls.** Added `scripts/herbal_control_0003_acquire.py`, a finite controller for the existing 216-source/150-MiB fetcher: at most 30 eight-file batches over eight hours, 12-minute spacing after success, 20-minute cooldown after HTTP 429 and stop after six rate limits. Launched it after the verified 37-source checkpoint; the initial command entered its cooldown, with no additional page yet attributed to it here. New synthetic controls: a 216-source/216-crop fake-manifest freeze passes then rejects a corrupted crop, and a 24-class Theil–Sen fit recovers a known affine map despite one outlier; qualified folio titles are rejected. Three focused tests, changed-file Ruff and `git diff --check` passed. The full repository suite passed **1,076 tests, eight skipped, 23 subtests in 99.14 seconds**. The real folio scorer correctly refused the absent full-input-freeze file. No paid API, new model training, folio development score, image feature or Voynich claim. Next: complete/push the current controller, folio challenger and test checkpoint before any folio score; let paced acquisition run, review each new drawing locally, then freeze all source/crop inputs and score development before evaluation.

## 2026-09-25 — NB-132: independent development scorer registered before any historical feature

**Question and rationale.** Asked whether the future 72-class historical-image score could be reproduced without trusting our own Hungarian assignment and threshold-selection implementation. The prospectively added auditor `scripts/herbal_control_0003_score_audit.py` (SHA-256 `dd9c700d40451f40401cc1780c627e0230e03179d6a0de040aaaf0b1997d3fd6`) imports neither production scoring code nor the solver. For a query that would otherwise be rejected at penalty `τ`, naming class `c` saves `τ − cost(q,c)`; a maximum-weight partial matching over positive saving edges, with each class used once, is exactly the registered minimum-cost reject assignment. NetworkX 3.6.1 supplies that different matching algorithm. The auditor independently rebuilds all six development distance medians, four cost families, 201-quantile-plus-extreme threshold grids, method choice, batch and independent-query decisions, known/unknown balanced accuracy, and next-distinct-prediction-pattern costs. It hash-checks the full input freeze and development crops, but only hashes the evaluation crop manifest without parsing its labels. The stage-2 registration now names this check explicitly; the original gate and split remain unchanged.

**Controls, observation and limitations.** Twenty random 4-query/3-class assignments and next-pattern costs matched exhaustive enumeration. A full synthetic 108-row development feature matrix was scored by the production code, then independently audited; all four method scores and the selected family agreed. Tampering with one stored normalization median was rejected. The two focused auditor tests passed in 18.92s after the final code change; changed-file Ruff and `git diff --check` passed. The full repository suite on the final auditor code passed **1,078 tests, eight skipped and 23 subtests in 82.95s**. The independent audit validates software arithmetic and tamper detection, not the historical chapter metadata, crop choices, Apple Vision feature quality, Voynich meaning or evaluation accuracy. No real HERBAL-CONTROL-0003 feature or threshold was computed.

## 2026-09-25 — NB-148: synthetic copy-mutate channel alignment defect and correction

**Question and prior basis.** Does the synthetic `L → C(L) → N(C(L))` generator actually preserve one mask bit per visible character when `copy_mutate` is selected, so prior reconstruction scores can be interpreted as recovering `C(L)`? The prior Voynich-use and theoretical basis are the previously registered EXP-0011/0014b/0016b/0023/0024/0026 synthetic deletion studies and `docs/research/PROTOCOL.md`; this block audits their existing data-generating channel, not a new architecture or historical hypothesis. Before work, read project `MEMORY.md`, recent notebook entries, the protocol, deep decipherment review, and those result/registration reports. The historical generator code at commit `480930b` had SHA-256 `07f39846ae1daad63019cb3baae0ccff5a99be05220c442d5913c0543bb35e50`.

**Actions and exact inputs.** Recleaned locally cached Project Gutenberg raw English #11, Latin #218 and Finnish #7000 with historical `clean_plaintext`; each output matched its saved `.clean.txt` byte for byte. Raw SHA-256 values were `01b38ea4c710a84bc18d0bd41271a5a1a92b94e97b2812f4dece97d4a694725e`, `84ac8411841a4d8f5f4a49b6a2cd1f466917c6a5af72916d5e0b2b1ecb2f659c`, `23e57fdc123b738f49e31a68ceef2b74b77a83baa0359ffd820f682905283631`. Used Python 3.12.13 / NumPy 2.5.3 / local `.venv`, seeds 4011 and 4012, rate 0.30, `random_char,periodic,copy_mutate`, train 4,000, validation 400, Finnish 300. Wrapped historical `insert_nulls` to record its input pre-null `C(L)` and raw output without changing RNG draws. Regeneration matched **every saved field** in all 4,700 persisted EXP-0023 rows and all three manifest hashes: train `2c824915…`, validation `7d572869…`, Finnish `cd72bf0f…`.

**Observation and independent checks.** `_mutate_token` sometimes emits two characters from a one-character copied token, while the historical loop appended only one mask bit/tag. Before 128-character normalization, `len(noisy) != len(mask)` in **842/842** train, **79/79** validation, and **100/100** Finnish WORLD_C copy rows; all corresponding stored targets, with padding spaces removed, fail to be prefixes of the pre-null `C(L)`. Random/periodic rows had **0/1,611**, **0/163**, **0/200** failures. Mean extra unlabelled characters in copy rows: 19.12, 18.86, 18.28. A separate 100-seed one-family invariant reproduction failed 100/100 copy and 0/100 random/periodic. The historical EXP-0024 oracle 1.0 is deletion with the same misaligned mask used to define the target, so it is a stored-mask identity, not a valid true-channel inverse. The exact counts apply only to replayed EXP-0023; other affected studies are traced by their code path and documented filler families, not individually recounted.

**Correction and validation.** Changed `insert_nulls` to account for every emitted character and consume the exact character null budget; it now asserts text/mask/tag length equality. `_fit_len` refuses an existing mismatch instead of concealing it. New regression tests check all six families × rates 0/0.3/0.7 × ten seeds, exact lengths/null counts/source subsequence/tag assignment, plus normalization refusal. A fresh full-size generation using the same sample counts and seeds under corrected code produced 813/73/90 copy rows in train/validation/Finnish (RNG paths differ). Across **all** generated WORLD_C rows, including those copy rows, there were zero raw-length, full-inverse, or scored-target-prefix failures. Focused tests passed 37/37; full suite passed **1,104 tests, eight skipped, 23 subtests in 121.77s**. Changed-file Ruff reports one pre-existing F841 unused `rng` in `latent_recovery.py:1513`; the new test file passes and the finding is unrelated to this correction. No paid API or training was run.

**Interpretation and next state.** Historical copy-containing scores/decisions stay archived with explicit errata in `docs/experiments/CHANNEL-ALIGNMENT-0001-erratum.md` and result reports. The easy-filler-only EXP-0012/0013/0014/0016 primary/0017 track is not implicated by this specific defect. This does not turn any failure into a success or support a Voynich null layer. Corrected data change the channel distribution and RNG trajectory, so do not overwrite old IDs or compare old scores as if labels alone changed. Next: prospectively register a fresh corrected-data study with an independent true-source oracle, bounded training/controls, and a new holdout before any renewed claim about copy-mutate transfer. Preserve unrelated EXP-0015 and TEACH working files.
